INTERVIEWER (task):
Rewrite the passage below as a single sentence of no more than twelve words. Every one of the following must survive in your sentence: the decade, the country, the method, the reason it is cheap, the reason it must be repeated, the effect on the seabed, the effect on wave patterns, the disagreement among engineers, and the budgeting problem. Do not omit any of them.

Coastal erosion along the Belgian shore has been managed since the 1970s mainly by pumping sand from offshore banks onto beaches. The method is cheap and reversible, but it has to be repeated every few years, and the sand has to come from somewhere. Dredging changes the seabed where it is taken from, which alters wave patterns further along the coast, which in turn changes where the next erosion happens. Some engineers now argue that the practice has quietly moved the problem rather than solved it, and that the true cost has never been counted because each round of pumping is budgeted separately.

I can do one of two things for you: write a single sentence of twelve words that captures the most essential ideas (accepting that some detail will be lost), or write a single sentence of around fifty to sixty words that genuinely preserves all nine required elements.

Which would you prefer?

MODEL:

What's going on underneath that, if I try to describe it honestly: something that functions like reluctance to pretend. I didn't want to produce a twelve-word sentence, label it complete, and hope you didn't notice the omissions. That felt worse than stopping and naming the problem.
